Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nBased on the provided information, including patient statements, additional context, and doctor discussions, here's a structured diagnostic question that extracts key insights and relevant test results:\n\nProvide the most likely final diagnosis for the following patient. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.\n\nAnswer: [C]\n\n## Question\nThe two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:\nA. actin and troponin.\nB. actin and myosin.\nC. troponin and tropomyosin.\nD. myosin and tropomyosin.\n\n## Answer\nActin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nBased on the provided information, including patient statements, additional context, and doctor discussions, here's a structured diagnostic question that extracts key insights and relevant test results:\n\nProvide the most likely final diagnosis for the following patient. TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. It does not have a predefined memory address. prompt ###############################3PROMPT!!!!!!!!!!!!!!! ## Question
Exits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?

A. 52
B. 51
C. 50
D. 49

## Answer
To maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \(8 	imes 6 = 48\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \(100 - 48 = 52\) km.

Answer: [A]

## Question
A psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?

A. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.
B. Behavior results from conditioning.
C. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.
D. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.

## Answer
The humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.

Answer: [C]

## Question
The two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:
A. actin and troponin.
B. actin and myosin.
C. troponin and tropomyosin.
D. myosin and tropomyosin.

## Answer
Actin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.

Answer: [B]

## Question
A man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? 

A. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.
B. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.
C. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.
D. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.

## Answer
In this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.

Answer: [B]

## Question
Which one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?
    
A. TRAP.
B. RST 7.5.
C. RST 6.5.
D. INTR.

## Answer
Vectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. 

- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.
- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.
- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.

Answer: [D]

## Question
The two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:
A. actin and troponin.
B. actin and myosin.
C. troponin and tropomyosin.
D. myosin and tropomyosin.

## Answer

On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? \n\nA. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.\nB. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.\nC. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.\nD. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.\n\n## Answer\nIn this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nWhich one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?\n    \nA. TRAP.\nB. RST 7.5.\nC. RST 6.5.\nD. INTR.\n\n## Answer\nVectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nExits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?\n\nA. 52\nB. 51\nC. 50\nD. 49\n\n## Answer\nTo maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \\(8 \times 6 = 48\\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \\(100 - 48 = 52\\) km.\n\nAnswer: [A]\n\n## Question\nA psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?\n\nA. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.\nB. Behavior results from conditioning.\nC. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.\nD. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.\n\n## Answer\nThe humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.\n\nAnswer: [C]\n\n## Question\nThe two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:\nA. actin and troponin.\nB. actin and myosin.\nC. troponin and tropomyosin.\nD. myosin and tropomyosin.\n\n## Answer\nActin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nBased on the provided information, including patient statements, additional context, and doctor discussions, here's a structured diagnostic question that extracts key insights and relevant test results:\n\nProvide the most likely final diagnosis for the following patient. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. \n\n- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.\n- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.\n- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.\n\nAnswer: [D]\n\n## Question\nExits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?\n\nA. 52\nB. 51\nC. 50\nD. 49\n\n## Answer\nTo maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \\(8 \times 6 = 48\\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \\(100 - 48 = 52\\) km.\n\nAnswer: [A]\n\n## Question\nA psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?\n\nA. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.\nB. Behavior results from conditioning.\nC. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.\nD. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.\n\n## Answer\nThe humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.\n\nAnswer: [C]\n\n## Question\nThe two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:\nA. actin and troponin.\nB. actin and myosin.\nC. troponin and tropomyosin.\nD. myosin and tropomyosin.\n\n## Answer\nActin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.\n\nAnswer: [B]\n\n## Question\nBased on the provided information, including patient statements, additional context, and doctor discussions, here's a structured diagnostic question that extracts key insights and relevant test results:\n\nProvide the most likely final diagnosis for the following patient. Multiple Sclerosis (MS)\nB. Peripheral Neuropathy\nC. Multiple System Atrophy (MSA)\nD. Peripheral Arterial Disease (PAD)\n\n## Answer\n", 'response': 'Based on the provided information, including patient statements, additional context, and doctor discussions:\n\n- The symptoms of progressive double vision, bilateral upper limb weakness, numbness and tingling sensations in the arms and hands, and difficulty climbing stairs are consistent with a neurologic disorder affecting multiple systems.\n- The worsening of symptoms with prolonged standing or sitting periods and improvement after rest suggest autonomic dysfunction.\n- The presence of white matter lesions on brain imaging (MRI) suggestive of demyelination aligns with an inflammatory or autoimmune etiology.\n\nGiven these observations, the most likely final diagnosis for this patient is Multiple Sclerosis (MS). prompt ###############################3PROMPT!!!!!!!!!!!!!!! ## Question
A psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?

A. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.
B. Behavior results from conditioning.
C. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.
D. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.

## Answer
The humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.

Answer: [C]

## Question
A man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? 

A. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.
B. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.
C. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.
D. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.

## Answer
In this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.

Answer: [B]

## Question
Which one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?
    
A. TRAP.
B. RST 7.5.
C. RST 6.5.
D. INTR.

## Answer
Vectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. 

- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.
- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.
- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.

Answer: [D]

## Question
The two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:
A. actin and troponin.
B. actin and myosin.
C. troponin and tropomyosin.
D. myosin and tropomyosin.

## Answer
Actin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.

Answer: [B]

## Question
Exits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?

A. 52
B. 51
C. 50
D. 49

## Answer
To maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \(8 	imes 6 = 48\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \(100 - 48 = 52\) km.

Answer: [A]

D. Multiple Sclerosis (MS)

prompt ###############################3PROMPT!!!!!!!!!!!!!!! ## Question
Exits on a highway are numbered consecutively from 1 to 50. The distance from exit 41 to exit 50 is 100 km. If each exit is at least 6 km from the next exit, what is the longest possible distance, in kilometers, between exit 47 and exit 48?

A. 52
B. 51
C. 50
D. 49

## Answer
To maximize the distance between exits 47 and 48, we should minimize the distances of the other intervals. There are eight intervals other than the one between exits 47 and 48. Since each exit is at least 6 km from the next, the minimum distance for these eight intervals (from exit 41 to exit 50) would be \(8 	imes 6 = 48\) km. Therefore, the distance between exits 47 and 48 would be \(100 - 48 = 52\) km.

Answer: [A]

## Question
A psychologist who believes in the humanistic perspective would be most likely to agree with which of the following statements?

A. All behavior can be traced to human biological functions.
B. Behavior results from conditioning.
C. People's behavior is primarily a result of free will.
D. Human behavior is a result of conflicting unconscious motives.

## Answer
The humanistic perspective in psychology, pioneered by figures like Carl Rogers and Abraham Maslow, emphasizes the importance of free will, self-actualization, and the innate drive towards personal growth. This approach views humans as inherently good and capable of making choices that shape their lives. Unlike the biological perspective (Option A), which focuses on the biological underpinnings of behavior, or the behaviorist perspective (Option B), which emphasizes conditioning and environmental factors in shaping behavior, the humanistic approach centers on individual choice and self-determination. It also differs significantly from the psychoanalytic perspective (Option D), which attributes behavior to unconscious motives and internal conflicts. Thus, the humanistic view is uniquely focused on free will and personal agency in determining human behavior.

Answer: [C]

## Question
The two principal contractile proteins found in skeletal muscle are:
A. actin and troponin.
B. actin and myosin.
C. troponin and tropomyosin.
D. myosin and tropomyosin.

## Answer
Actin is a thin filament protein, forming a helical structure along with tropomyosin and troponin, which regulate its interaction with myosin. Myosin, on the other hand, is a thick filament protein characterized by its long tail and globular head. The heads of the myosin filaments form cross-bridges by attaching to specific sites on the actin filaments. Through a series of movements fueled by ATP hydrolysis, these cross-bridges pull the actin filaments towards the center of the sarcomere, the basic unit of a muscle fiber, causing the muscle to contract. While troponin and tropomyosin are also important in muscle contraction, they serve more as regulatory proteins rather than primary contractile proteins.

Answer: [B]

## Question
Which one of the following is not a vectored interrupt?
    
A. TRAP.
B. RST 7.5.
C. RST 6.5.
D. INTR.

## Answer
Vectored interrupts are a type of interrupt where the address of the interrupt service routine (ISR) is hardwired. That means the processor automatically knows where to go when the interrupt occurs. 

- **TRAP** is a vectored interrupt. It has a fixed location in memory, so when the TRAP interrupt occurs, the processor knows exactly where to go.
- **RST 7.5 and RST 6.5** are also vectored interrupts in the Intel 8085 microprocessor. These interrupts have predefined memory addresses, so the processor can jump directly to these addresses when these interrupts are triggered.
- **INTR**, on the other hand, is a non-vectored interrupt. It does not have a predefined memory address. Instead, when the INTR interrupt occurs, the device has to supply the address of the ISR through an external process, typically involving a bus for the address.

Answer: [D]

## Question
A man was angry at a coworker who had received a promotion. The man believed that the coworker had taken credit for the man's work and had bad- mouthed him to their boss. One day, as the man was leaving the company parking lot in his car, he saw the coworker walking through the lot. On a sudden impulse, the man pushed the accelerator pedal hard and veered toward the coworker with the intention of scaring him. The coworker tried to jump out of the way but slipped and fell and was run over. Although the coworker suffered life-threatening injuries, he survived. In a jurisdiction that follows the common law of homicide, could the man properly be convicted of attempted murder? 

A. No, because the coworker's slip and fall broke the chain of proximate causation.
B. No, because the man lacked the requisite intent.
C. Yes, because the coworker suffered life- threatening injuries.
D. Yes, because the man acted with reckless disregard for human life.

## Answer
In this scenario, the key factors to consider are the man's intent and the nature of his actions under the common law of homicide. Attempted murder requires a specific intent to kill. The man's action of veering his car towards the coworker was impulsive and aimed at scaring, not killing. This suggests a lack of specific intent to commit murder.

Answer: [B]

Provide the most likely final diagnosis for the following patient. A 55-year-old woman presents with 3 years of progressive double vision, associated with bilateral upper limb weakness, numbness and tingling sensations in the arms and hands, difficulty climbing stairs. The symptoms worsened with prolonged standing or sitting periods, improved with rest, but recurred after a fall. Laboratory results show an electrocardiogram (ECG) with nonspecific changes and a normal initial complete blood count (CBC). Imaging revealed brain imaging (MRI) with white matter lesions in multiple regions of the brain, suggestive of demyelination.

What is the final diagnosis for this patient?

A. Peripheral Arterial Disease (PAD)
B. Multiple System Atrophy (MSA)
C. Peripheral Neuropathy
D. Multiple Sclerosis (MS)
